bluetooth: Add permission check to scan start

WebBluetoothServiceImpl::RequestScanningStart did not call
GetBluetoothAllowed(), allowing documents where Bluetooth is disallowed
by Permissions Policy or embedder policy to initiate BLE scans and show
the scanning prompt.

This CL adds the missing GetBluetoothAllowed() check to
WebBluetoothServiceImpl::RequestScanningStart, returning early with the
result if Bluetooth access is not permitted.

Fixed: 572227416

777c5895d07ace2ffc5d5c9f0734dcf2a805900c [ diff ] 
[🌷] Deflake DownloadLocationChangeEnd2EndTest on Automotive

DownloadLocationChangeEnd2EndTest previously triggered downloads using
test.gzip (application/gzip), which ChromeDownloadManagerDelegate
intentionally blocks on Android Automotive because application/gzip is
not a Blink-supported MIME type. Switch TEST_FILE to superbo.txt
(served with Content-Type: text/plain and Content-Disposition:
attachment) and remove @DisableIf.Device(DeviceFormFactor.AUTOMOTIVE)
so the download location dialog tests run across all form factors.

Bug: b:572086975
Cq-Include-Trybots: luci.chromium.try:android-14-automotive-landscape-x64-rel

Add internal Blink web tests for `navigator.playHaptics()`

Haptic output is invisible to web pages, so standard web tests cannot
check whether calls reach the browser. Intercepting `HapticsService`
with MojoJS lets Blink verify renderer dispatch, gating, clamping, and
input validation without hardware.

bluetooth: Remove should_unbond_on_error from ConnectToServiceInsecurely
📋 Metadata

53964c8206fdd5f5fc046b1ed930db7e944932b3

author

Sat Nov 21 06:53:25 2026
tree
07861c08ff8384e39ab1552c951d0d54a0a3844d
parent
fe19ac36b308d69d0757154b409926f0bee356e9 [ diff ] 
bluetooth: Remove should_unbond_on_error from ConnectToServiceInsecurely

should_unbond_on_error was introduced in crrev.com/c/3850666
(crbug.com/204274786) to recover from a "half-paired" state in Phone Hub
and Nearby Connections on ChromeOS, where the Chromebook retained a bond
to an Android phone that had lost its pairing keys. When true, BlueZ
connection errors (br-connection-refused, br-connection-canceled, or
kReasonUnknown) on a bonded device triggered device->Forget().

However, ConnectToServiceInsecurely is exposed to the untrusted Nearby
utility process and only validates the service UUID, not the target
device address. Furthermore, on Floss, all failed RFCOMM socket
connections return a generic error string ("Socket failed to connect")
that maps to kReasonUnknown, causing any failed connection attempt to
unbond any paired device (such as a keyboard, mouse, or headphones).
Because ConnectToServiceInsecurely cannot distinguish a half-paired
phone from another bonded peripheral that does not host the requested
service, remove should_unbond_on_error and stop calling Forget() on
connection failure.

[heap] Add Oilpan-safe blink::StableSort() for HeapVector
📋 Metadata
commit
602f078195d0770e23f6797b026ab75d4f6b4f20
[ log ]
author
Omer Katz <omerkatz@chromium.org>
Sat Nov 21 06:36:52 2026
committer
Chromium LUCI CQ <chromium-scoped@luci-project-accounts.iam.gserviceaccount.com>
Sat Nov 21 06:36:52 2026
tree
17e50770f4bb9315efe6c7696a4774593bd49d0f
parent
fcca3a45e71473086030af9ef1d0534b2ab1f46b [ diff ] 
[heap] Add Oilpan-safe blink::StableSort() for HeapVector

std::stable_sort and std::ranges::stable_sort allocate an off-heap
temporary buffer (of std::unique_ptr<T[]>) and move elements into
it during merge steps. When sorting HeapVectors of Member<T> or
traceable DISALLOW_NEW() part objects, those GC references are
temporarily moved off-stack and off-heap, hiding them from
conservative stack scanning and Oilpan write barriers.

This CL introduces and uses blink::StableSort() which computes a
stable index permutation via std::sort and permutes the elements in
place.

[blink-gc] Fix std::stable_partition() and gmock GC plugin violations

Avoid off-heap std::unique_ptr allocations holding Oilpan references in
builds without Clang modules:

- Replace std::stable_partition() + erase()/Shrink() on HeapVector with
  in-place EraseIf() in ComputeIntersectionList() and
  ImageDecoderExternal::MaybeSatisfyPendingDecodes(), avoiding the
  off-heap std::unique_ptr<T[]> temporary buffer allocated by
  std::stable_partition().
- Replace testing::Return() holding a GarbageCollected
  MockMediaStreamTrack pointer in TransferredMediaStreamTrackTest with
  lambdas that allocate the mock on invocation instead of storing it in
  ReturnAction's std::unique_ptr.

5c7518a7b06bc2610bb8c711af66967d9fef8f13 [ diff ] 
WebNN: Enable OpenVINO NPU logging with --webnn-ort-logging-level

Debugging the OpenVINO NPU plugin requires setting its own LOG_LEVEL in
the OpenVINO EP's load_config. Today that means editing the source,
since the load_config session config entry is fixed by WebNN.

Reuse --webnn-ort-logging-level instead. When the logging level is INFO
or VERBOSE, ApplyEpConfigEntries sets NPU.LOG_LEVEL to LOG_INFO in the
OpenVINO EP's load_config at runtime, so the JSON in config_entries
stays the single source of truth.

LOG_INFO gives the NPU compiler version, target device ID, compile time,
peak memory, blob size and batching decisions. LOG_DEBUG and LOG_TRACE
add per-pass and per-inference tracing, which is too noisy to be useful
here. LOG_LEVEL is only set for NPU because the GPU plugin does not
support it and rejects unknown options. It only affects logging, not the
compiled blob, so it is safe to apply in both the Compiler process and
the GPU process that imports the compiled model.

Ideally the EP would derive LOG_LEVEL from the session logging severity;
a TODO tracks removing this once ORT supports that.

Also add IsOrtInfoLoggingEnabled() and use it wherever the INFO or
VERBOSE level is checked, so these checks stay consistent.

Web Serial: Fix Bluetooth permission bypass

Previously, persistent Bluetooth serial permissions only verified the
device MAC address. This allowed a site with permission to one service
on a device to access all services on that device.

This CL fixes this by storing and verifying the canonical Bluetooth
service class ID (128-bit UUID) in the permission object, and forming a
composite key in GetKeyForObject to prevent key collisions across
multiple services on the same physical device.

Additionally, GetObjectDisplayName is updated to include the service
name or identifier (localized name if available, 16-bit short UUID, or
128-bit UUID) to allow users to distinguish between multiple services on
the same device in Site Settings.

Note: Updating IsValidObject to require a non-empty device path and a
canonical Bluetooth service UUID intentionally invalidates legacy
Bluetooth serial permissions that lacked service-level scoping,
preventing overbroad access.

Bug: 521010314

Mark Storage#key() and Storage#getItem() with [Affects=Nothing]

Allow DevTools side-effect-free evaluation (throwOnSideEffect: true) on
Storage#key() and Storage#getItem() (matching the default-safe named
interceptor on Storage) instead of aborting with EvalError, per Blink's
[Affects=Nothing] contract:
https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/bindings/IDLExtendedAttributes.md;l=566-580

with SideUiCoordinator.

 was created, so getView() would return null during registration.
-Now, mRootView and its layout params are initialized before
 registering with SideUiCoordinator.

updater: match install-source pings and update checks with json::

ExpectInstallSource() matched `eventtype` 2 and `installsource` with two
separate content regexes, which could match different apps. Give it the
app id and match both inside that app, comparing `installsource`
exactly instead of case-insensitively. The offline-install tests pass
the app that they install.

ExpectUpdateCheckRequest() only absorbs an update check, so any app may
carry it. Match an app with an empty `updatecheck` in `request.apps`
instead of the `"updatecheck":{}` content regex.

updater: match the app of expected uninstall pings

ExpectPing() matched any request with an event of the expected type,
so an expectation for the updater's uninstall ping also accepted an
app's uninstall ping, and the other way around. Give ExpectPing() an
app id and match the event inside that app with json:: matchers.

ExpectUninstallPing() and ExpectUserUninstallPing() now expect the
updater's own ping. A new ExpectAppUninstallPing() expects the ping that
the updater sends when it removes an app's registration, which
UninstallCompanionAppWhenUninstallUpdater expects for its app.

46363390cd1abcb384fedd02c815e6c9f868ef78 [ diff ] 
[infobars] Prevent global infobar collapse and reflow on tab switch

Populate active global infobars onto the incoming active tab in
Browser::OnActiveTabChanged() before BrowserWindow::OnActiveTabChanged()
swaps InfoBarContainer's InfoBarManager, rather than waiting until
did_active_tab_change_callback_list_ notifies subscribers afterwards.

Previously, InfoBarContainer::ChangeInfoBarManager() attached to an
empty incoming ContentInfoBarManager, hiding the container (0px) and
expanding ContentsWebView, only for BrowserInfoBarManager's post-swap
callback to add a new instance via OnInfoBarAdded() (animate = true) and
force a second WebContents reflow on every tab switch.

Also track ShowGlobally() activation order in a FIFO vector so tab
switches and new windows preserve show order instead of sorting by
InfoBarIdentifier enum value.

browser_infobar_manager.h/cc getting large. A follow CL will decompose
it.

tree
9a9bb9e65d7305ec9066dfaa00108b52c42437ea
parent
4708eeed253ef71650458800a9b80651ae903d3a [ diff ] 
updater: check GetPoliciesJson() results with json:: matchers

The GetPoliciesJson integration test walked the parsed JSON with nested
CHECK_DEREF() calls, so a missing key or a value of the wrong type
crashed the test, skipping its cleanup, instead of failing it. Check the
same values with json::HasPath() matchers, which report the path of
each missing or different value.

Also wait for the result with a TestFuture and check it outside the
callback. A JSON parse failure used to leave the run loop waiting until
it timed out; now it fails the test right away and prints the result.
TestFuture also ignores a late reply after a timeout, where the old
callback used a destroyed run loop. Since the matchers now apply to JSON
other than request bodies, broaden the json_matchers.h file comment.

[PasswordManager] Remove AccountInfo::IsValid() in filter unittest

`AccountInfo::IsValid()` is deprecated in favor of checking the specific
fields required at each callsite.

Remove the redundant `DCHECK(account_info.IsValid())` and
`CHECK(account_info.IsValid())` calls in
`multi_profile_credentials_filter_unittest.cc`, where all core and
extended `AccountInfo` fields are already populated by
`MakeAccountAvailable()` and `AccountInfo::Builder`.

Settings: Fix event listener race in SettingsUIToolbarAndDrawer.

In "app drawer closes when exiting narrow mode", the test triggers the
drawer open and close animations and yields to the event loop before
starting to wait for the completion events. If the animation finishes
while yielded (for example on slower builds), the event fires before the
listener is attached and the test times out. This race was introduced in
crrev.com/c/8540330 and is currently blocking crrev.com/c/8688384 on
linux-rel.

Register the event listeners before opening and closing the drawer,
matching the preceding "app drawer" test.

TAG=agy
CONV=3b414a4b-1130-4b8a-a560-ad5b5a499e45

d8bd7c079ea84e810ea53faab27569664b9b8b75

d99206f697e30ef6151bbb67548fecd2cd1391a7 [ diff ] 
[SAA] Enforce is_storage_access_potentially_allowed in Network Service

Currently, only credentialless iframes and fenced frames convey their
unpartitioned storage restrictions to the Network Service (via a nonce
in CookiePartitionKey and IsolationInfo). Other restricted contexts,
such as sandboxed iframes without
allow-storage-access-by-user-activation or frames blocked by the
storage-access Permissions Policy, are not distinguishable in the
Network Service, allowing a compromised renderer to opt into an existing
Storage Access API grant or activate Storage Access Headers.

Note that this enforcement operates per URLLoaderFactory and per
RestrictedCookieManager (as well as per trusted navigation request). If
a restricted frame shares a renderer process with an unrestricted
same-origin execution context (for example, a frame restricted only by
allow="storage-access 'none'"), a compromised renderer could still use
the unrestricted context's Mojo endpoints; cross-site embedders and
origin-sandboxed frames are process-isolated under Site Isolation.

This CL adds a trusted `is_storage_access_potentially_allowed` boolean
to `URLLoaderFactoryParams`, `TrustedUrlRequestParams`, and
`NetworkContext::GetRestrictedCookieManager()` (defaulting to `true`).
When this boolean is false (and the
`kEnforceStorageAccessApiRestrictions` feature is enabled):
- `CorsURLLoaderFactory::IsValidRequest()` and
  `RestrictedCookieManager` reject calls with a non-`kNone`
  `storage_access_api_status` via `mojo::ReportBadMessage()`.
- `CalculateCookieSettingOverrides()` and
  `RestrictedCookieManager::GetCookieSettingOverrides()` omit
  `kStorageAccessGrantEligible`.
- `CookieSettingsBase::GetStorageAccessStatus()`, `CorsURLLoader`,
  `NetworkServiceNetworkDelegate`, and
  `URLLoader::ShouldSetLoadWithStorageAccess()` disable Storage Access
  Headers (`Sec-Fetch-Storage-Access: none`, ignoring `retry` and
  `load`).

No production behavior changes in this CL yet because all callers pass
`true`; follow-up CLs will populate the flag from the browser process.

🌺 Disable FindRequestManagerTest#RectsUpdateWhenMainFrameSizeChanged

The following test failed all retry attempts (hard-failed) in 3/3 recent
builds on android-14-tablet-landscape-arm64-rel (#11963, #11965, #11966)
and 1/1 recent build on android-arm-tests (#41363), with 46 CI and 138
Try unexpected verdicts over the past 30 days.

Disabled here:
  FindRequestManagerTest.RectsUpdateWhenMainFrameSizeChanged (Android only)

Example failing run:
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/android-14-tablet-landscape-arm64-rel/11965

Why disable rather than revert:
Chronically racy find-in-page rect comparison on Android (hard-failing
on android-14-tablet-landscape-arm64-rel #11963/#11965/#11966 and
android-arm-tests #41363; 46 CI + 138 Try unexpected verdicts over 30d):
FindInPage::FindMatchRects reuses cached per-frame match rects when
version is unchanged while recomputing ActiveFindMatchRect() live across
async scroll/resize updates, causing EXPECT_EQ(new_rects[i],
delegate()->active_match_rect()) mismatches at
find_request_manager_browsertest.cc:930.

To run/deflake this test on CQ before re-enabling, add the following
footer(s) to your CL description:
  Cq-Include-Trybots: luci.chromium.try:android-14-tablet-landscape-arm64-rel
  Include-Ci-Only-Tests: chromium.android:android-14-tablet-landscape-arm64-rel|content_browsertests

This is mitigation, not a fix. The bug tracks the real investigation and
is assigned to the owning team.

Avoid anti-aliasing straight edges in CustomCornersBackground
📋 Metadata
commit
a61e461835d7a50fef094f1d539355847d635c36
[ log ]
author
Thomas Anderson <thomasanderson@chromium.org>
Sat Nov 21 03:03:04 2026
committer

Sat Nov 21 03:03:04 2026
tree
a360bb3d1200073b8be491bccbf793752f1e9758
parent
b35afdae55af0a687c58ecdb12c29f18db8fcb6a [ diff ] 
Avoid anti-aliasing straight edges in CustomCornersBackground

CustomCornersBackground::Paint drew the view's entire bounding rounded
rect with anti-aliasing enabled. At fractional scale factors on
non-pixel-canvas platforms (such as Linux with fractional text scaling),
anti-aliasing the straight edges of the local bounds produces partial
alpha along the boundary pixel row when a scaled edge does not land on
an integer pixel. Adjacent views or childless solid-color separator
layers snap their edges to the nearest integer pixel, leaving a 1px
semi-transparent seam at the toolbar boundary where the translucent
window background or desktop shows through.

When the corner radii fit within the view bounds, clip out only the
rounded corners with anti-aliasing and paint the rectangular bounds
without anti-aliasing so straight edges snap to integer pixels.

108a6de063d534f75249a110395d57085524704c [ diff ] 
Fix flaky SearchApiTest.Normal by listening before tabs.create()

The test created a tab and only added its tabs.onUpdated listener in
the tabs.create() callback. Listener registration is an asynchronous
IPC to the browser, so when the NTP finished loading before the
browser saw the new listener, the "complete" event was never
dispatched and QueryPopulatedDispositionEmpty (and the two other
subtests using the same pattern) hung until the RunLoop timeout.

Register the listener before creating the tab and match the created
tab's id once tabs.create() returns.

UTR runs of SearchApiTest.Normal (--test-launcher-retry-limit=0):
  linux-arm64-rel-fyi:         before 4/50 failed (4/50 with 60s
                               RunLoop timeouts); after 0/100
  linux-arm64-wayland-rel-fyi: before 5/50 failed (3/50 with 60s
                               RunLoop timeouts); after 0/100
Every failure hit the full RunLoop timeout, so raising timeouts does
not help. The same flake shows on x64 Linux Tests (passes on retry).

f441bcc4d9db757437976a738bf897b0dd00df90
parent
3caf6fa2d6256a9074e6b10edf499e31a92c0e09 [ diff ] 
[blink] Pin parkable script text across threads

Parkable strings and uncached external V8 strings rely on main-thread
task serialization so that main-thread aging never discards an
uncompressed character buffer mid-access. Passing a parkable module
script directly to an off-main-thread worklet allowed main-thread aging
to free the backing buffer while the worklet isolate was still reading
it.

Convert parkable script sources to non-parkable copies on the main
thread before sending module creation parameters across threads, and
reuse that stored non-parkable copy when notifying waiting worklet
clients rather than creating a fresh wrapper per client. Because a
parkable string only discards its buffer when it holds the sole
reference to its underlying string buffer, wrapping that shared buffer
in a non-parkable copy pins it while off-thread references exist and
allows main-thread parking to resume once they are destroyed.

Non-parkable wrappers hold an immutable string buffer, so they are also
safe to inspect in memory snapshots from any thread and to wrap as
normal cacheable external V8 strings, restricting uncached external
string resources strictly to parkable strings on the main thread.

f5a9eabd84ddd17a1d9d05450456dffe61a9e653
parent
9da260bdde68b3a47c7568b03f7621f264afa843 [ diff ] 
[Blink] Preserve parser provenance for script CSP

Preparing a disconnected parser-created script clears its
parser-inserted scheduling state. Reconnecting it can then incorrectly
grant it the strict-dynamic treatment intended for dynamically created
scripts.

Retain CSP-specific parser provenance when a script with a source
attribute or nonempty inline text is prepared while disconnected in
its parser document. Use it for inline CSP checks and external or module
fetches without changing script scheduling. Preserve legitimate execution
for nonce-authorized and dynamic scripts, inert templates, and initially
empty scripts populated later.

Add regressions for parser removal through selectedcontent and controls
for authorized execution, with and without CSP.

parent
1a8f7e756c9ce85eef7c54b495c1a4f6ed33bbef [ diff ] 
updater: replace the event_regex test parameter with an app matcher

ExpectUpdateSequence() and ExpectInstallSequence() took `event_regex`,
a content regex that the event ping had to match. Only InstallId set
it, to check the app's `iid`. Replace it with `ping_app_matcher`, an
optional json::ValueMatcher that the ping's entry for the app must
match. The change covers those functions, the IntegrationTestCommands
interface, and its system and user implementations. InstallId passes
json::HasKey("iid", "my_install_id") instead.

The check is stricter: the regex matched case-insensitively anywhere in
the raw ping body, while InstallId's matcher compares the `iid` value
exactly, in the same app entry as the expected event.

Also document both functions, and add argument-name comments to the
bool arguments at the call sites that this change touches.

Add comprehensive unit tests for cross-world Resource::CanReuse

This CL replaces `ResourceTest.CanReuseServiceWorkerResource` with a
table-based `ResourceTest.CanReuseAcrossWorlds`. The new test checks
`Resource::CanReuse()` for each combination of the cached resource's
world, how its response was obtained, and the new request's world.

This adds unit test coverage for:
- The catch-all rejection of cross-`WorldForCsp()` reuse
  (`kPreventAllCrossWorldForCspReuse`).
- The rejection of cross-`TargetWorld()` reuse
  (`kPreventAllCrossTargetWorldReuse`).
- The Service Worker specific rejection
  (`kCrossWorldServiceWorkerResourceMismatch`).
- Network and Service Worker redirects.

This is a follow-up for https://crrev.com/c/8433306 and
https://crrev.com/c/8391784.

tree
502fafbf53a713ca07f8f41ce4b37c73774a279e
parent
99e76b624a33d9079fe4ae2aac8a34779ccb42e8 [ diff ] 
Retain transfer buffer in UsbDeviceHandleWin::Request

In UsbDeviceHandleWin::GenericTransfer, the transfer buffer was passed
both as an argument and moved into the completion callback within the
same call to MaybeStartWatching(). Because function argument evaluation
order is unspecified, moving the buffer into the bound callback could
occur first, leaving Request::buffer_ unset while the overlapped I/O
operation was in flight.

Update Request to take sole ownership of the transfer buffer for the
duration of the overlapped operation and pass the buffer back through
Request::StartWatchingCallback once completed. This ensures the buffer
is reliably held by the Request object for the full lifecycle of the
pending I/O.

f561b79f8ea549b7e344e8adc5d8a5a857f17aa0
parent
23766e0a53683298dde197bb1c322721e6fa8830 [ diff ] 
Skip ::before/::after on images showing primary content

An <img> showing its image is a LayoutImage, which can't have children,
so its ::before and ::after never generate a box; they only render for
fallback content. With rules like img::before { content: "" }, a common
way to style broken images, every loaded image still created both
pseudo-elements and resolved their style.

Don't generate them while the image shows primary content. Switching to
or from fallback content changes the inherited UAShadowHostData of the
image, so style recalc adds or removes them.

The SkipImageBeforeAfterForPrimaryContent runtime flag is a kill switch.

3dc2fa3906f1c4f55b6144f6baf1c4314d4be9ca [ diff ] 
[remoting] Add in-memory PerfettoTraceProducer

Adds `PerfettoTraceProducer` (`perfetto_trace_producer.{h,cc}`) to
`//remoting/base:perfetto_tracing` to record in-process `TrackEvent`s
strictly in memory and return serialized `.pftrace` bytes
asynchronously:

- Manages a single active `perfetto::TracingSession` per producer with
  `StartBlocking()`, `StopAndFlush()`, and `ReadTrace()` chunk assembly.
- Releases producer ownership before invoking `TraceDataCallback` so
  callers can start a new session from within the callback.
- Defers `Session::Cancel()`, `~Session()` auto-cancellation,
  `config.duration`, stop-timeout protection, and `SetOnErrorCallback`
  handling to the follow-up CL.

Test: remoting_unittests --gtest_filter=PerfettoTraceProducer*

agents: Add Android/Java review rules to knowledge_base

## wnwen (human)

Upstream some rules.

## Agent generated details

Add 11 rules to agents/knowledge_base/rules/general/java/. Each comes
from review comments that Chromium reviewers left repeatedly on Clank
(Chrome for Android) CLs in November 2026, or from a policy already
written in a Chromium doc (third_party/jni_zero/README.md,
styleguide/java/java.md). They come from
the Clank review agents' rule set, which was compared rule by rule with
the existing knowledge base; rules that the knowledge base already
covers or that contradict Chromium docs were dropped.

NullAway and JNI Zero:
- use-assumenonnull-only-before-immediate-dereference
- omit-redundant-null-asserts-on-jni-pointer-receivers
- prefer-jni-pointer-wrappers-over-long-native-pointers
- prefer-jni-zero-wrappers-over-legacy-java-calls

Tests:
- regression-tests-must-fail-without-the-fix
- keep-public-transit-station-references-fresh
- trigger-state-changes-inside-public-transit-trips
- annotate-test-widened-members-with-visiblefortesting

API, lifecycle and style:
- add-callers-for-new-apis-in-the-same-cl
- clear-handlers-on-outliving-views-in-destroy
- import-classes-instead-of-fully-qualified-names

COMMON_METADATA lists the new rules in the existing Java, JNI and
NullAway capability groups, and adds two groups: legacy Java_* calls in
C++ and Public Transit tests under javatests/.

Today only base/android/DIR_METADATA mixes in
general/java/COMMON_METADATA, so these rules reach base/android/ only.
A follow-up CL will propose the mixin for chrome/android/ and
chrome/test/android/ to their owners.

Bug: none

Audit RAII classes and counters in core/html and core/dom for best practices.

This audits RAII classes and nesting counters for three things:
 (1) use of [[nodiscard]] to ensure variables are named
 (2) use of STACK_ALLOCATED() (which in some cases allows removing GC
     workarounds that were required because it was missing)
 (3) CHECK()s for overflow/underflow

This removes the unused DocumentLifecycle::DeprecatedTransition class
rather than fixing it.

tree
e7b029d21469d355987134b157748d55e6439e93
parent
6e044650a8b68bc441964b2113295996dc1fe889 [ diff ] 
Fix flaky InstanceIDApiTest.GetCreationTimeFormat

getCreationTime() returns base::Time::InMillisecondsFSinceUnixEpoch(),
which keeps sub-millisecond precision, while Date.now() in the renderer
returns whole milliseconds (gin::TimeClamper floors, and within 5us of
the next millisecond, or 100us on Android, may round up). When getID()
and getCreationTime() finish within the same millisecond, the creation
time is greater than the later Date.now() value, e.g. on
linux-arm64-wayland-rel-fyi build 5656 the test got
1794867578191.249 with an upper bound of 1794867578191.

Compare the upper bound at millisecond granularity instead, and allow
1ms of slack on the lower bound since Android's creation time is a
floored integer millisecond that a rounded-up Date.now() can exceed.
The test still catches a creation time in seconds, which is what it is
for.

The test has been flaky on linux-arm64-wayland-rel-fyi (10 failed, 73
flaky in 30 days) and on the x64 Linux builders (Linux Tests,
linux-wayland-mutter-rel-tests, linux-wayland-weston-rel-tests).

20af802ee9994ec890eaa9f412e73c01de789035 [ diff ] 
base/i18n: fix ICU4X Transliterator FFI bugs and use pre-baked rules

Clean up and harden the `BUILD_RUST_TRANSLIT` implementation in
`//base/i18n` ahead of enabling it by default:

1. Fix LSan memory leak in `ICU4XTransliterator`: store
   `rust::Box<translit_ffi::TransliteratorWrapper>` directly instead of
   leaking `.into_raw()` into a `raw_ptr` with a `= default` destructor,
   and drop the unused `raw_ptr.h` include from `transliterator.h`.
2. Fix out-of-bounds / UTF-8 `cxx` FFI safety bugs in
   `transliterator.cc`: pass `std::string_view` arguments using
   `base::StringViewToRustSlice` / `StringViewToRustStrUTF8` /
   `RustStrToStringView` instead of calling `rust::Str(sv.data())`
   (which scans for a NUL byte past `sv.size()`).
3. Guard `<unicode/translit.h>` and `unicodestring.h` behind
   `#if !BUILDFLAG(BUILD_RUST_TRANSLIT)` (with a
   `#if UCONFIG_NO_TRANSLITERATION` compile-time guard in the `#else`
   branch), assert `!enable_rust_translit` on Cronet, and exclude
   `transliterator.{cc,h}` and `transliterator_unittest.cc` on Cronet
   (`!is_cronet_build`, which has zero `Transliterator` callers) so
   `UCONFIG_NO_TRANSLITERATION=1` can be enabled in `//third_party/icu`
   without affecting Cronet's dependency closure.
4. Replace `RuleCollectionProvider`,
   `TransliteratorMultiSourceProvider`, and the obsolete
   `LowercaseTransliterator` override with an explicit transform-ID
   allowlist plus pre-baked BCP-47-T `TransliteratorRulesV1` lookups in
   `//third_party/rust/icu4x_data` (including Chromium's
   `und-t-und-x0-*` custom rule sets), fixing inverted `Hira-Kana` /
   `Kana-Hira` short aliases and dropping `icu_properties` and
   `icu_provider` from `i18n_transliterator_ffi` `deps`.
5. Remove the now-unused `CreateTransliteratorFromRules` API from
   `base/i18n/transliterator.{h,cc}` after all callers migrated to
   `CreateTransliterator`.
6. Expand `transliterator_unittest.cc` across `Hira-Kana` / `Kana-Hira`
   short aliases, non-null-terminated `std::string_view` slices,
   unpaired UTF-16 surrogates, and invalid ID `EXPECT_DCHECK_DEATH`
   checks.

parent
89fa0a4ac0636925a9679ed0bbd6915baa301865 [ diff ] 
[ContextualTasks] Hide picker for ineligible tabs

List only tabs whose page context eligibility last resolved to
`kEligible` in the Contextual Tasks tab picker and when deciding whether
to show its button, so the picker no longer appears when the only other
tabs are pages that cannot be shared. Tabs already attached to the
session stay listed so the user can still unselect them.

`PageContextEligibilityHelper` now remembers each tab's last `kEligible`
or `kNotEligible` result, and the picker uses it while a reloaded page
is `kUnknown`, so pages whose `<meta>` tags decide eligibility no longer
flash the picker. Tab observers refresh the picker and open tab pickers
only when that last known result changes.

tree
5f2f8e96ff44f98014eddc2a7d059654c8475d44
parent
c813799b9d1be316441333823b1cf97a0a5291af [ diff ] 
Don't inherit initiator origins in unassigned SiteInstances

Empty document schemes other than about: (e.g., chrome-native: on
Android) never assign a site, so they commit in an unlocked process.
They could still inherit an opaque origin with the initiator's
precursor, which then gets added to that process's committed origins.

This CL gives these navigations an opaque origin with no precursor and
adds a CHECK that nothing committed in an unassigned SiteInstance has a
precursor. This is a step towards disallowing committed origins in
unused ProcessLocks.

332e8c206a0d5b070860e6cc819dff7ee1667ba2 [ diff ] 
Chrome Copy Links: add TabCopyLinks to the field trial testing config

Enables `kTabCopyLinks` in fieldtrial_testing_config.json on desktop
platforms (chromeos, linux, mac, windows) so the bots exercise the "Copy
Link(s)"(http://goto.google.com/chrome-copy-links) tab and tab group
menu items ahead of the M160 Finch experiment.

Explicitly disables `kTabCopyLinks` in the disabled-state tests so they
do not rely on the feature being off by default.

22d0fcda799f6e6cd506d32c1d070f5dcab8513f [ diff ] 
Deflake ExtensionApiTest.DisplayModeWindowIsInFullscreen on Wayland

The test checked the display-mode media feature from window resize
events. On Wayland, a late resize from entering fullscreen can reach
the renderer after the page has already called restore(), so the
"restored" check runs while the display mode is still fullscreen and
fails ("Display mode of the restored window is 'standalone'").

Wait for the display-mode media query to change instead of relying on
the resize event order.

linux-arm64-wayland-rel-fyi via UTR, --gtest_repeat, retries off:
- before: 1/100 failed with default timeouts, 6/100 with raised
  timeouts (--ui-test-action-max-timeout=60000), all ~0.5 s
  assertion failures, so a timeout increase doesn't help
- after: 0/300 failed

Also flaky on linux-wayland-mutter-rel-tests and
linux-wayland-weston-rel-tests.

Cq-Include-Trybots: luci.chromium.try:linux-arm64-wayland-rel-fyi

tree
460e99b99e5c12b1aa410ef0f370d648a6090855
parent
cfd48e27af8ee48cb27a6335136dd71c994b7e22 [ diff ] 
[remoting] Limit the size of event channel messages

HostEventDispatcher now drops event channel messages larger than 4 KiB
without parsing them. Key, mouse, ten-point touch, and sentence-length
text events are each estimated to be under 1 KiB, so this leaves
headroom for legitimate clients. The limit is a default based on that
estimate and has not been measured against every client.

Dropped messages are logged at most once every 10 seconds. A TODO
covers telling the client when input or clipboard data is dropped.

Messages received while no InputStub is set are now dropped. This was
previously only checked by a DCHECK.

This is the first of a series that bounds the size and rate of remote
input events.

9d6f7e5edf31a498ca7795d4a2d1b761564487df [ diff ] 
updater: match more test request bodies with json:: matchers

Replace the content regexes that check requests for a known app with
json:: matchers in integration_tests_impl.cc and integration_tests.cc.
Each event must now be in the `events` of the app it belongs to. The
matchers also no longer depend on the order or neighbors of keys,
compare values other than app ids case-sensitively, and explain
mismatches by path.

Also remove the GetContentMatcher({""}) matchers from the CRX download
expectations, since an empty regex matches any body, and make the
SameVersionUpdate response list its app in `apps`, the protocol V4 name,
instead of `app`, which the parser ignores.

The `event_regex` parameter, ExpectPing(), ExpectInstallSource(), and
ExpectUpdateCheckRequest() still use regexes, since they don't name an
app.

parent
fe37240e64795875bce62233c922a94ba2d7b943 [ diff ] 
[Reading mode PDFs] Replace `BuildPageTreeTime` with microsecond version

Switch the heuristic page tree build timer to
`UmaHistogramMicrosecondsTimes()`, since ~70-79% of pages fall under 1
ms. Move the timer to `PdfAccessibilityTreeBuilder` so it also covers
ruling-line extraction, text-run splitting, table detection, and
`NameHeadingsFromContents()`.

`BuildPageTreeTime` isn't kept alongside, since it times a narrower span
and can't be compared directly. This feature is only in beta so nothing
in production is affected.

OBSOLETE_HISTOGRAM[Accessibility.PDF.Heuristic.BuildPageTreeTime]=Replaced by Accessibility.PDF.Heuristic.BuildPageTreeTime2, which uses microsecond granularity and covers full heuristic page setup and post-processing.

0e7dd53e00969b511e92df450325752f58856d20
parent
b4f9dc3bf42c7f22524add6625795402551dd417 [ diff ] 
[webauthn] Open caBLE tunnels on the frame's network context

ChromeAuthenticatorRequestDelegate gave the FIDO discovery factory the
system network context, so caBLE tunnels to the tunnel server ignored
the profile's network configuration, such as a proxy set by an
extension or policy. The factory now returns the requesting frame's
storage partition network context.

Profile network contexts require a NetworkAnonymizationKey, so
FidoTunnelDevice now opens its WebSocket with an internal-request
IsolationInfo for the tunnel server's origin, as the enclave WebSocket
does.

parent
2f8d1c875b85bc9aec96ad0346cd11e242201359 [ diff ] 
[ttc] Use SuspendType::kUI for media tools and guard null browser

PlayVideo and PauseVideo previously passed
MediaSession::SuspendType::kSystem, causing OnResumeInternal(kSystem)
to return early whenever the user paused playback via page controls
(kContent) or media keys (kUI). Switch both to SuspendType::kUI so
playback resumes reliably and routes through navigator.mediaSession
action handlers.

Also guard against null browser() and page_context_monitor_ pointers
across AiOverlayTools so tool calls fail cleanly without crashing
when no browser window or page context monitor is available.

Bug: 587935179
Test: browser_tests --gtest_filter=AiOverlayToolsBrowserTest.*

[webapps] Migrate WebApp and callers to ManagementData [4/N]

Replace `WebApp::sources_` and
`WebApp::management_to_external_config_map_` (along with
`ExternalManagementConfig` and `ExternalConfigMap`) with a single
`ManagementData management_data_` member, remove the stale
`is_uninstalling_` persistence comment, and add the optional
`management_data` field (tag 84) to `proto::WebApp` inside
`LINT.IfChange(MemberVariables)`. Callers read per-source management
state via `management_data()` and mutate via `WebApp` methods so
cross-field `WebApp` invariants stay enforced; unused
`WebApp::AddExternalSourceInformation()` is removed.

Update `WebAppRegistrar` (including `HasExternalAppWithInstallSource()`
checking `management_data().HasSource()`),
`ExternallyManagedAppManager` (checking `kSourcesWithPlaceholder` when
`install_placeholder` is set), `DedupeInstallUrlsCommand`,
`FinalizeInstallOrUpdateJob` (`WriteManagementData()`),
`RemoveInstallUrlJob`, `RemoveWebAppJob`, `WebAppPolicyManager`,
`web_app_helpers.cc`, test utilities (`AddSource` in
`AddInstallUrlData` and `kSourcesWithPlaceholder` guard in
`AddInstallUrlAndPlaceholderData`), tests (including switching six
`ExternalAppResolutionCommandBrowserTest` placeholder cases from
`kInternalDefault` to `kExternalPolicy`), and `AsDebugValue` JSON test
expectations to use `ManagementData`.

Add a temporary v7 bridge in `web_app_database_serialization.cc`
translating between `proto::WebApp` v7 fields (`sources` and
`management_to_external_config_info`) and `ManagementData` while making
`ProtoToWebAppManagement()` return
`std::optional<WebAppManagement::Type>`. A follow-up CL upgrades
`WebAppDatabase` to v8 and switches serialization natively to
`proto::ManagementData`.

activity_reporter: Set first-time DateLastActive in ReportActive()

Set the browser's DateLastActive to "first time" right before the
first report of a session, instead of when the reporter is constructed.
To reach the persisted data then, the reporter keeps its configurator.
The testing constructor creates one too, so tests can check the marker,
and CreateActivityReporterForTesting() now needs the update_client
prefs registered.

No behavior change in practice: the browser creates the reporter on
first use, and every caller calls ReportActive() on it right away. This
prepares for reporting custom tab use under its own app id
(crrev.com/c/8817646), whose marker should only be written when a
custom tab is reported.

5cf4707ec0152e975ed6831004d926e971de3a6f
parent
e49b8f7727e7470e3f7413a01b39e83419814244 [ diff ] 
[ContextualTasks] Attach context to voice queries

Query submission requires a recent user interaction within 5 seconds so
background scripts cannot exfiltrate attached tabs or Lens regions,
assuming the submit click is the final input event. However, for a voice
query, clicking the microphone is the last hardware input before the
user speaks and the query submits itself after a delay, causing context
to not be uploaded since the user interaction took to long.

To fix this, the context is uploaded if there is an active or recently
active audio stream. Consuming both the interaction timestamp and the
audio capture token on submission prevents duplicate context reads when
parallel speech and waveform audio streams close asynchronously after
submit.

5e689848b26b60aba83c7ccc0520fe2ce35090f9
parent
c93727248d2b5a322c214cc76dec4be87e8e8ec9 [ diff ] 
[iOS] Initialize `source_browser` in `MoveTabGroupToBrowser()`

In `MoveTabGroupToBrowser()`, `source_browser` is declared without an
initializer and only assigned inside the loop if one of `browsers`
contains `source_tab_group`. In normal calls where `source_tab_group`
belongs to a browser in the same profile, `source_browser` is assigned
before `if (!source_browser)` is evaluated, so the code works as
expected.

If `source_tab_group` is not found (e.g. an invalid group or
cross-profile move), reading `source_browser` is theoretically undefined
behavior in C++, though in Chrome uninitialized stack pointers are
`nullptr` (`0`) in official builds without `DCHECK` and
`0xAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA` when `DCHECK` is enabled or in non-official builds.
Consequently, official non-`DCHECK` builds take the `if
(!source_browser)` early return as intended, whereas
`DCHECK`/non-official builds evaluate `!source_browser` as `false` and
crash when dereferencing `source_browser->GetProfile()`.

Initialize `source_browser` to `nullptr` so the `if (!source_browser)`
guard works reliably in all builds.

Bug: none

[remoting] Drain job completion port via sentinel I/O packet
📋 Metadata
commit
f53b183ebe05445599c47bef78a990d8e66abb3b
[ log ]
author
Joe Downing <joedow@google.com>
Sat Nov 21 01:09:25 2026
committer
Chromium LUCI CQ <chromium-scoped@luci-project-accounts.iam.gserviceaccount.com>
Sat Nov 21 01:09:25 2026
tree
7902c45ab985b9285e1de39ac447ca1fe0ba4dba
parent
5ee54ff5c7292522cf65584b7ec7a9e09016ff08 [ diff ] 
[remoting] Drain job completion port via sentinel I/O packet

WtsSessionProcessDelegate::Core registers itself as the raw completion
key for its Windows Job Object on io_task_runner_. Previously, posting
a regular task to drain notifications on Stop() could run ahead of
queued completion-port packets in MessagePumpForIO (and could re-enter
on duplicate ACTIVE_PROCESS_ZERO tasks), freeing Core while
kernel-queued job packets still referenced it.

Replace the drain task with a JobDrainState state machine and use
IoCompletionPortDrainer to drain the completion port once
ActiveProcesses reaches zero and the job handle is closed. Because
completion-port packets are dequeued FIFO, Core is released only after
all job packets already queued when the sentinel is written have been
dispatched.

Additional hardening:
- Initialize() now fails if the drainer pipe cannot be created.
- Failed job/drainer registration on the I/O thread now reports a fatal
  error on the caller thread instead of leaving launches pending.
- The fallback poll timer re-issues TerminateJobObject() while active
  processes remain, logs once, and backs off its poll interval.

Fixed: 587008025

M133 (Copy as image) and its chrome://flags entry expired in M136.
Inline the feature and its parameters in C++ while keeping WebUI
loadTimeData behavior unchanged for follow-up cleanup:

- Remove kLensOverlayImageContextMenuActions, its FeatureParams, and
  their getter helpers from components/lens/lens_features.
- Remove the expired enable-lens-overlay-image-context-menu-actions
  flag entry, descriptions, and metadata.
- Replace IsLensOverlayCopyAsImageEnabled() and
  IsLensOverlaySaveAsImageEnabled() with their launched literal values
  (true and false) in LensOverlayUntrustedUI.

370d8a0c1c4b13f0d62c69130aa9abbb75d8233b [ diff ] 
[XDeviceSignin] Exclude history cross-device signin promo from Android

The cross-device sign-in promo on chrome://history is a Desktop-to-Phone
flow that opens a Desktop Views QR-code bubble via SigninViewController.
On Android, the Mojo interface was nevertheless compiled, bound, and
backed by a no-op stub.

Exclude the promo from Android builds: gate the
history_cross_device_signin_promo mojom, its TypeScript bindings, and
the promo localized strings on !is_android / !BUILDFLAG(IS_ANDROID),
the promo element/CSS/tests on !is_chromeos && !is_android (GN / grit
<if>), and the HistoryUI binder and handler member on
BUILDFLAG(ENABLE_DICE_SUPPORT). This removes the no-op
AndroidHistoryCrossDeviceSigninPromoHandler, the
history::CreateCrossDeviceSigninPromoHandler platform indirection, and
the Android-only HistoryUITest.AndroidSigninPromoCardReturnsFalse that
covered the stub. HistoryUI now creates
HistoryCrossDeviceSigninPromoHandler directly.

accd5968aa02f64f8961c229d4419917f0fdfa04
parent
20593e81f4363471cf34ce2bce441e5f1ad4d4ff [ diff ] 
[remoting] Add IoCompletionPortDrainer helper

Add a Windows helper class that writes a 1-byte sentinel packet to a
dedicated, non-inheritable overlapped pipe registered on a
MessagePumpForIO thread's completion port.

Posting a regular task to an I/O SingleThreadTaskRunner does not drain
queued completion packets because MessagePumpForIO can run posted tasks
ahead of packets still waiting in the kernel completion port queue.
Because completion-port packets are dequeued FIFO, the drainer's
callback runs only after every previously queued completion packet has
been dispatched.

[Tab Groups] Use `IcuBridge::ListFormatter::FormatUnitList()`

`GetTabListString()` in `tab_group_accessibility.cc` builds the list of
tabs (e.g. "3 tabs, • Tab 1, • Tab 2, • Tab 3") included in the
accessible name of a tab group header and its hover card. It used a
private copy of the ICU `ListFormatter` boilerplate with the unit list
pattern, to avoid an "and" before the last item; replace it with
`base::i18n::IcuBridge::ListFormatter::FormatUnitList()` via
`base::i18n::IcuBridge::GetInstance().list_formatter()`.

This is a refactor with no behavior change: both create the ICU
`ListFormatter` for the default locale with `ULISTFMT_TYPE_UNITS` and
`ULISTFMT_WIDTH_WIDE`, so the accessible name is unchanged in every
locale. The only difference is the ICU-failure path, which used to
return an empty string and now joins the items with ", ".

In `//chrome/browser/ui/views/tabs/groups:impl`, replace the
`//third_party/icu` dependency with `//base:i18n`, since no file in that
target uses ICU directly anymore.

The new
`TabGroupHeaderViewTest.HoverCardAccessibilityText_AcrossLocales` pins
the accessible name of an unnamed group in `en-US`, `fr`, `de` and the
right-to-left `ar`, switching the locale with
`ui::test::ScopedApplicationLocale`; it checks that the tabs are joined
with the unit list pattern of each locale (e.g. "et" before the last tab
in French). For `ar`, POSIX non-Mac/non-Android builds prepend RLM
(`\u200F`) via `l10n_util::AdjustParagraphDirectionality()` to each RTL
localized string (`IDS_GROUP_AX_LABEL_UNNAMED_GROUP_FORMAT`,
`IDS_TAB_GROUPS_UNNAMED_GROUP_HOVER_CARD_HEADER`,
`IDS_GROUP_AX_LABEL_EXPANDED`), so the expectation guards that with
`#if`. `zh-CN` and `ja` are not tested because their unit list pattern
has no separator or only a space, which this refactor does not change.

Screenshots of the tab group header accessible name in the native
accessibility tree (`chrome://accessibility`), captured on Linux from
`browser_tests` with `--lang`. The accessible name is identical before
and after, as expected for a refactor; only unrelated accessibility node
ids differ:
- `en-US` before:
  https://drive.google.com/file/d/1GA_PdnnXC1lvV402IiyQBUkbm5p9HSxx/view
- `en-US` after:
  https://drive.google.com/file/d/1KP9uQK-UnefJAPlyev8PlSVNBJUHgiVs/view
- `zh-CN` before:
  https://drive.google.com/file/d/1lctYXy6KVLKviLvS5oG8TSGXc_rLETKB/view
- `zh-CN` after:
  https://drive.google.com/file/d/1__eola0hDZd6rknzAaSy6anTOA9TIO_j/view
- `ja` before:
  https://drive.google.com/file/d/1J2N7lDIcOXEhjZwEG0SSsPXG56gspW0A/view
- `ja` after:
  https://drive.google.com/file/d/1wNFWyVxXO_UfybwulP5-A0uwfrbKyucu/view

Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter=TabGroupHeaderViewTest.*

e3d5db53d9a146ccf96b349a2fdc22808519aa2a

c9fb918d76dba746d15ddcbb714f397455b0ac68 [ diff ] 
[glic] Forward throttling_profile_id in GlicCicProxyURLLoader

When DevTools or CDP emulates network conditions (such as Fast 4G or
offline via `Network.emulateNetworkConditions`), Chromium attaches
`throttling_profile_id` to navigation and subresource requests and
routes them through `network::ThrottlingController`.

Previously, `GlicCicProxyURLLoader` constructed a fresh upstream
`network::ResourceRequest` without copying `throttling_profile_id`. As a
result, proxied `#glic-cic` requests (both `/glic` navigations and
`/cic/*` subresources) bypassed CDP network throttling entirely.

Because `network::ThrottlingController` is a network-service-wide
singleton keyed by `throttling_profile_id`, forwarding a non-empty
`original_request.throttling_profile_id` to `upstream_request` applies
offline, latency, download, and upload throttling across the proxy's
storage partition boundary. Cross-origin `devtools_request_id`,
`devtools_stack_id`, `devtools_observer`, and `cookie_observer` remain
unset so raw upstream headers, corp SSO redirects, and `Set-Cookie`
headers are never exposed to the guest's DevTools session. Empty
`base::UnguessableToken` values are skipped to avoid Mojo
deserialization errors.

Adds a `SetUpstreamFactoryForTesting` hook to
`GlicCicProxyingURLLoaderFactory`, unit tests covering navigation and
subresource field forwarding and offline errors, and a deterministic CDP
`Network.emulateNetworkConditions` browser test.

TAG=agy

tree
d669dda20f65075074cc1f41c5c5886ca4aca177
parent
8077c2cba99f521abd03ec613eda680ac99093e9 [ diff ] 
media: Use kRGB_888x and kBGR_888x for XBGR and XRGB frames

Skia now has kBGR_888x_SkColorType, which is to kBGRA_8888 what
kRGB_888x is to kRGBA_8888.

Use it and kRGB_888x in GetSkInfoForFormat() for PIXEL_FORMAT_XRGB and
PIXEL_FORMAT_XBGR, instead of mapping those formats to the 8888 color
types with an opaque alpha type.

Related cleanups:
- VideoPixelFormatFromSkColorType() now maps the 888x color types to
  XBGR and XRGB.
- WebCodecs VideoFrame::copyTo() to an opaque format (RGBX/BGRX) draws
  into the matching color type with alpha, unpremultiplied, then
  converts in place to the opaque color type. This drops alpha without
  premultiplying the color.
- RasterInterface::ReadbackARGBPixelsAsync() accepts the 888x color
  types, since WebCodecs background readback of XRGB/XBGR frames now
  requests them.

Bug: None

tree
6908f378319f818d5bcf7fe3e40498c658dafedd
parent
df1750420da02da082db93352f730109632e2ff4 [ diff ] 
🛡 [FRE] Share the paused-state touch filter with the lightweight FRE

FirstRunActivity drops touch events while it is not resumed on Android
versions before T, because the platform can still deliver touches to a
paused activity underneath a non-touchable overlay. The filter lived
in FirstRunActivity only, on the assumption that it was the sole First
Run surface; LightweightFirstRunActivity (ToS dialog for WebAPKs/TWAs)
shares the base class but not the filter, so its primary button could
receive touches while paused.

Move the filter into FirstRunActivityBase so that every First Run
activity consumes touch events unless it is RESUMED, and add a
Robolectric test for the lightweight activity mirroring the existing
FirstRunFilterTouchUnitTest.

69d403ef42f4638feaa0f1a05dad2b4eba7e6366

81d2a58182f015f8ac93d174e719c442e8843f4c [ diff ] 
[reader mode] Validate frame origin and font scale for distiller prefs

Enforce primary main frame, committed `chrome-distiller://` URL, and
`chrome-distiller://` origin checks in BindDistillerJavaScriptService
before binding DistillerJavaScriptService or updating the UI handle, and
scope DistillerJavaScriptServiceImpl to the current document via
content::DocumentService. Align renderer-side context creation checks in
DistillerJsRenderFrameObserver to verify both document URL scheme and
security origin protocol.

Raise kMinFontScale from 0.4f to 0.5f (50%) to match the minimum Reader
Mode font scale UI boundary, and reject non-finite or out-of-bounds font
scaling values across DistillerJavaScriptService,
WebDistillerViewerSource, DistillerNativeJavaScript, and
DistilledPagePrefs.

aff6fbda870851200d19705486824db1c0d81c0a
parent
4ae4f731833d23060911aef04a82ef95f09b50c7 [ diff ] 
🛡 [Android] Attribute device-usage notifications to the committed URL

The camera/microphone, Bluetooth, USB and serial notifications on
Android named the site using Tab.getUrl(), i.e. the visible URL. While
a browser-initiated navigation is pending, the visible URL is the
pending destination, but the device access that triggers the
notification is held by the still-committed document, so the
notification could name a site that never had the device. Once shown,
the media notification was only refreshed when the set of media types
changed, so a URL change with the same media types kept the previous
text.

Pass WebContents.getLastCommittedUrl() (falling back to Tab.getUrl()
without WebContents) from TabWebContentsDelegateAndroidImpl, record
only committed navigation URLs in TabWebContentsObserver for the
destroy path, and make MediaCaptureNotificationServiceImpl rebuild a
notification when its URL changes, not only its media types. This
matches the desktop MediaStreamCaptureIndicator, which already uses
the last committed URL.

cdf9180af6f5679d635fe81abbf8d61a91041c69

b8e32f8fa9c99c267f8fe646980cd15d6a09b701 [ diff ] 
[Extensions] Let ExtensionSettings block installs by internal permission

Some extension capabilities change core browser behavior without any
permission that the developer declares in the manifest. For example,
the chrome_settings_overrides manifest key can replace the default
search provider, homepage, and startup pages, and chrome_url_overrides
can replace the New Tab page. Chrome tracks these capabilities as
internal API permissions, but the ExtensionSettings policy cannot match
on them: it parses blocked_permissions and allowed_permissions with
kDisallowInternalPermissions, so these entries are silently ignored.

As a result, enterprise admins have no reliable way to keep this class
of extension from being installed without side effects. The current
options are to lock the affected browser settings for every user, or
to keep per-extension blocklists that always lag behind new extensions.

Let blocked_permissions and allowed_permissions name an allowlist of
internal permissions that map directly to manifest keys:
- searchProvider, homepage, startupPages (chrome_settings_overrides)
- newTabPageOverride (chrome_url_overrides)

Extensions that hold a blocked permission can't be installed, and
installed ones are disabled, the same as for any other blocked
permission. The policy stays backward compatible for these permissions.
Other internal permissions, such as tabCaptureForTab, are still
ignored, and extensions still cannot request internal permissions in
their manifest permissions lists.

Also let SettingsOverrideAPIPermission::FromValue accept entries with
no value, since policy lists name a permission without the URL detail
stored in prefs.

Bug: b:369167208
Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter=*InternalPermission*:*SettingsOverride*

SplitView: Add SplitViewEnabledFn

SplitViewEnabledFn is the sole authority for checking whether Android
Split View is enabled. By consolidating all conditions in a single
class, we can maintain a single source of truth for all callers
(including native callers).

For native callers, we'll add a JNI bridge for them when needed. This
way both the Java side and the native side will use the same set of
conditions (including the cached Java feature flag).

parent
44c1bcd1c15632e6a96d4f4ea8753371c9d2a13a [ diff ] 
[ttc] Trace TTC tool calls to their effect

The MES client already reports when a tool call arrives and when its
response is sent. This fills in what happens in between, so a tool call
that "worked" but did nothing visible can be told apart from one that
really took effect.

A new TtcToolCallTracer, owned by SessionControllerImpl while the TTC
debug tools are on, traces the session's tool calls into its
TtcDebugSession. ToolController tells it when a tool call starts
running, which tab it acts on, and when it finishes. Each is one call
where ToolController already handles that step: RunToolCall(),
PerformActionOnTrackedContents() and OnToolCallDone(). Every tool that
acts on the voice-focused tab runs through
PerformActionOnTrackedContents(), so ToolController keeps no list of
such tools, and a tool added later is covered without further changes.
Browser-level tools such as switch_tab and open_known_page don't run
through it and have no target. Neither do get_screenshot and
get_pointer, which only read the tab.

TtcDebugSession takes the target as a report of its own, OnToolTarget(),
instead of with the dispatch, to match. OnToolNoTarget() is removed: a
tool that finds no tab to act on fails, and that failure is already
recorded as the tool's error. The result comes in as OnToolSucceeded()
or OnToolFailed(), each with what it needs, instead of OnToolFinished()
with an ok flag. A failure also clears an effect that was already
reported missing, e.g. by a navigation that committed an error page,
since the error explains it. A background target is now only flagged
when the tool is dispatched: a tool that opens a new tab, such as
open_url with new_tab, leaves the tab it acted on behind it, so
finishing in the background is expected.

A response to a call that never ran (rejected as busy, cancelled after
an earlier call failed, or with malformed arguments) is now recorded as
a rejection, with the error from the response, rather than left
pending. OnToolCallRejected(), which nothing called, is removed.

For tools that should change their tab, the tracer starts a
TtcToolEffectVerifier on the target. It keeps each pending tool until
both its result and its effect are in, since the effect often lands
before the actor reports back, and snapshots the target tab before and
after.

The tracer also observes the actor journal and records what the actor
framework logs for the session's own actor task, on its browser and
renderer tracks. What TTC logs on its own tracks is already recorded
directly.

The tracer lives exactly as long as the TTC session, while the record
is kept after it for review. When the session ends, the record now
marks tool calls that were still running as interrupted, and stops
showing effects that were still being checked, instead of leaving them
looking like they never finished.

All of this only runs while the TTC debug tools are enabled: without
them there is no tracer and ToolController skips the reporting.

Tests:
- TtcToolCallTracerTest: result/effect ordering, a tool that succeeds
  without effect, failed tools (also after an error page), a target
  recorded for whichever tool reports one, tools without a target,
  which actor journal tracks are recorded, and nothing recorded once
  the tracer is gone.
- TtcDebugSessionTest: tool calls in flight when the session ends, a
  failure explaining a missing effect, responses to calls that never
  ran, and a background target only flagged at dispatch.
- TtcDebugPipelineBrowserTest: an open_url call traced to its effect, a
  failed tool call, and switch_tab/open_known_page succeeding without a
  target.

Part 13 of the chrome://ttc-internals series. Design: go/ttc-internals
